\displaystyle=\xi^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{B}-\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\xi^{\gamma}M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\xi^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{B}-\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\xi^{\gamma}\big(\bar{M}^{\mu\nu}{}_{\gamma}+P^{\mu\nu}{}_{\gamma}\big)\,,

(B.2.16)

as given in (
4.4.30
) and (
4.4.34
).

Appendix C
Equations of motion

In this appendix we derive the equations of motion for the various (coordinate basis) theories studied throughout the thesis. This begins with the Einstein actions, first for the Levi-Civita connection and then in the metric-affine setting. We then show the derivation for the modified field equations of the metrical
f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})
theories and the metric-affine
f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})
theories.

C.1
Einstein actions

C.1.1
Levi-Civita Einstein action

Here we calculate the field equations for the Levi-Civita Einstein action (
4.1.51
), making use of the objects
M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\lambda}
(
4.1.14
) and
E^{\mu\nu\lambda}
(
4.1.1
). These calculations are originally to be found in our work
[
222
]
.
The metric variation of the Einstein action
S_{\textrm{E}}
and the matter action
S_{\textrm{M}}[g_{\mu\nu},\varPhi^{A}]
can be written as

\displaystyle\delta S

\displaystyle=\delta S_{\textrm{E}}[g_{\mu\nu}]+\delta S_{\textrm{M}}[g_{\mu\nu},\varPhi^{A}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int d^{4}x\Big[\delta\sqrt{-g}\mathbf{G}+\sqrt{-g}\delta\mathbf{G}+2\kappa\frac{\delta S_{\textrm{M}}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}\delta g^{\mu\nu}\Big]\,,

(C.1.1)

with
\mathbf{G}
defined in (
4.1.8
). Focusing on the gravitational action, and temporarily dropping the factor of
2\kappa
, we can write

\delta S_{\textrm{E}}=\int\delta\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}\,d^{4}x+\int\sqrt{-g}\ \delta g^{\mu\nu}(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}\,d^{4}x\\
+\int\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}\,d^{4}x\,,

(C.1.2)

where
(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}
stands for
\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\lambda\mu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\nu\sigma}-\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}
. The first two terms are

\displaystyle\int\delta\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}d^{4}x

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int\sqrt{-g}\ \delta g^{\rho\sigma}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}(\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\lambda\mu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\nu}-\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\lambda})d^{4}x\,,

(C.1.3)

\displaystyle\int\sqrt{-g}\ \delta g^{\mu\nu}(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}d^{4}x

\displaystyle=\int\sqrt{-g}\ \delta g^{\rho\sigma}(\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\lambda\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\sigma}-\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\lambda})d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.4)

Next we expand
g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}
to arrive at

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}

\displaystyle=g^{\mu\nu}\big(\delta\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\lambda\mu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\nu}+\delta\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\lambda\mu}-\delta\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}-\delta\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=g^{\mu\nu}(\delta^{\sigma}_{\mu}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\kappa\nu}+\delta^{\sigma}_{\nu}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\kappa\mu}-\delta^{\rho}_{\mu}\delta^{\sigma}_{\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}-\delta^{\sigma}_{\kappa}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\mu\nu})\delta\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=M^{\rho\sigma}{}_{\kappa}\delta\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\rho\sigma}\,.

(C.1.5)

Here we have introduced the object

\displaystyle M^{\alpha\beta}{}_{\gamma}:=\frac{\partial\mathbf{G}}{\partial\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}}=2g^{\nu(\beta}\Gamma^{\alpha)}_{\gamma\nu}-g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\lambda}-g^{\mu\nu}\delta^{(\beta}_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\alpha)}_{\mu\nu}\,,

(C.1.6)

which is constructed to be symmetric over its first two indices to match the symmetry of the connection. This is also given in equation (
4.1.14
).

Expanding the variation of the Christoffel connection in terms of metric variations gives

\displaystyle\delta\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}(g_{\rho\lambda,\sigma}+g_{\sigma\lambda,\rho}-g_{\rho\sigma,\lambda})+\frac{1}{2}g^{\kappa\lambda}(\delta g_{\rho\lambda,\sigma}+\delta g_{\sigma\lambda,\rho}-\delta g_{\rho\sigma,\lambda})

\displaystyle=\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}g_{\zeta\lambda}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\kappa\lambda}(\delta g_{\rho\lambda,\sigma}+\delta g_{\sigma\lambda,\rho}-\delta g_{\rho\sigma,\lambda})

\displaystyle=\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}g_{\zeta\lambda}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\kappa\lambda}(\delta g_{\alpha\beta,\gamma}\Delta^{\alpha\beta\gamma}_{\rho\sigma\lambda})\,,

(C.1.7)

where we also define

\displaystyle\Delta^{\alpha\beta\gamma}_{\rho\sigma\lambda}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\{\lambda}\delta^{\beta}_{\rho}\delta^{\gamma}_{\sigma\}}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\delta^{\beta}_{\rho}\delta^{\gamma}_{\sigma}+\delta^{\alpha}_{\sigma}\delta^{\beta}_{\lambda}\delta^{\gamma}_{\rho}-\delta^{\alpha}_{\rho}\delta^{\beta}_{\sigma}\delta^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\,,

(C.1.8)

which simply permutes indices using the
Schouten bracket
, see
[
225
]
. Again we note that only the symmetric part over the indices
\alpha\,\beta
of
\Delta^{\alpha\beta\gamma}_{\rho\sigma\lambda}
in (
C.1.7
) contributes.

Putting the above equations back into the integrand, we can write the last term of (
C.1.2
) as

\int\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}\,d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}M^{\rho\sigma}{}_{\kappa}\delta\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\rho\sigma}\,d^{4}x\\
=\int\sqrt{-g}M^{\rho\sigma}{}_{\kappa}\big[\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}g_{\zeta\lambda}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\kappa\lambda}(\delta g_{\alpha\beta,\gamma}\Delta^{\alpha\beta\gamma}_{\rho\sigma\lambda})\big]\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.9)

To simplify the second term, we make use of the object
E^{\mu\nu\lambda}
(
4.1.1
), which is the permutation of
M^{\rho\sigma\lambda}
given by

E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}:=\Delta^{\alpha\beta\gamma}_{\rho\sigma\lambda}M^{\rho\sigma\lambda}=M^{\beta\gamma\alpha}+M^{\gamma\alpha\beta}-M^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\\
=2g^{\nu\alpha}g^{\beta\mu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}-2g^{\gamma(\alpha}g^{\beta)\mu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\lambda\mu}+g^{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\gamma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\lambda\mu}-g^{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\,.

(C.1.10)

In equation (
4.1.20
) we show this to be the variation of
\mathbf{G}
with respect to the partial derivative of the metric. We then have

\displaystyle\int\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}\Big(\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}g_{\zeta\lambda}M^{\rho\sigma}{}_{\kappa}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\delta g_{\alpha\beta}\Big)\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.11)

Integrating the second term by parts and dropping the boundary terms, then rewriting the metric variation in terms of the inverse metric gives

\int\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}\,d^{4}x=\int\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big[\sqrt{-g}g_{\zeta\sigma}M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\mu\nu}\\
+\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\rho}g_{\beta\sigma}\partial_{\gamma}(\sqrt{-g}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma})\Big]\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.12)

As we wish to obtain the Einstein tensor, we will simply expand everything in terms of the connection and its derivatives.
The first term in the integrand of (
C.1.12
) can be expanded as

\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\sqrt{-g}\,g_{\zeta\sigma}M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\mu\nu}=\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\sqrt{-g}\Big(2g^{\nu\eta}g_{\sigma\zeta}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\rho\eta}\\
-g^{\mu\nu}g_{\sigma\zeta}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\lambda\rho}-g^{\gamma\eta}g_{\sigma\zeta}\Gamma^{\zeta}_{\mu\rho}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\eta}\Big)\,.

(C.1.13)

The second term of (
C.1.12
) involves taking the partial derivative of
E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
, which leads to the following expression

\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}g_{\alpha\rho}g_{\beta\sigma}\partial_{\gamma}(\sqrt{-g}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma})=\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big(2\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\rho\sigma}-2\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\gamma\rho}-g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\\
-2g_{\alpha\rho}g^{\epsilon\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\gamma\epsilon}+g_{\alpha\rho}g^{\eta\gamma}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}+g_{\alpha\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\gamma\sigma}+g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\eta\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\eta}\\
+\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}-\partial_{\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\kappa\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Big)\,.

(C.1.14)

The full
(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}
variation becomes

\int\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}\delta(\Gamma_{\mu\nu})^{2}\,d^{4}x=\int\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\sqrt{-g}\Big(2\Gamma^{\eta}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}-2\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\gamma\rho}+g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\eta\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\eta}\\
-g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}+\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}-\partial_{\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\kappa\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Big)\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.15)

Putting together equations (
C.1.3
), (
C.1.4
) and (
C.1.15
), we get a final expression for the variation of the Einstein action

\delta S_{\textrm{E}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big[\Gamma^{\eta}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}-\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\gamma\rho}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\eta\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\eta}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\\
+\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}-\partial_{\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\kappa\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Big]\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.16)

The terms in the square brackets are understood to be symmetrised over the indices
\rho\,\sigma
. We then recognise this to be the Einstein tensor,

\displaystyle\delta S_{\textrm{E}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\big(R_{\rho\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}R\big)\,d^{4}x=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}G_{\rho\sigma}\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.17)

The metric variations of the full action (
C.1.1
) is then

\displaystyle\delta S=\delta S_{\textrm{E}}[g_{\mu\nu}]+\delta S_{\textrm{M}}[g_{\mu\nu},\varPhi]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big(G_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{2\kappa}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S_{\textrm{M}}}{\delta g^{\rho\sigma}}\Big)\,d^{4}x

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.1.18)

where defining the metric energy-momentum tensor as

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S_{\textrm{M}}[g_{\mu\nu},\varPhi^{A}]}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}\,,

(C.1.19)

leads to the Einstein field equations

G_{\rho\sigma}=\kappa T_{\rho\sigma}\,.

(C.1.20)

C.1.2
Metric-affine Einstein action

For the metric-affine Einstein action, or Einstein-Palatini action, given in equation (
4.4.44
), we now have an independent metric and affine connection. Beginning with the metric variations, a direct calculation gives

\displaystyle\delta_{g}S_{\bar{\textrm{E}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\delta(\sqrt{-g}\bar{\mathbf{G}})\,d^{4}x

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\biggl\{-\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\mu\nu}g_{\mu\nu}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\Big[\delta g^{\mu\nu}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\nu}\big)\\
+\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)\big(\partial_{\nu}\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}\delta(g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})\big)\Big]\biggr\}\sqrt{-g}d^{4}x\,,\end{multlined}

which then becomes

=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\mu\nu}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\nu}\big)\Big)d^{4}x\\
+\textrm{`surface terms'}-\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}\Bigl(\sqrt{-g}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)\Bigr)d^{4}x\\
-\frac{1}{4\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)\big(\delta g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}+g_{\alpha\beta}\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)d^{4}x\\
-\textrm{`surface terms'}+\frac{1}{4\kappa}\int\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}\Bigl(\sqrt{-g}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\Bigr)d^{4}x\,,

(C.1.23)

where the surface terms come from integration by parts. Discarding these boundary terms leaves the expression

\delta_{g}S_{\bar{\textrm{E}}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\sigma\gamma}\Big[-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\gamma}g^{\mu\lambda}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\lambda}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)\\
-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\gamma}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)\big(\partial_{\nu}g^{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)\\
+\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\sigma\gamma}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\sigma\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\gamma}\big)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\nu}\Big(\sqrt{-g}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\gamma}\delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\sigma\gamma}\big)\Big)\\
-\frac{1}{2}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g_{\sigma\gamma}-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\gamma}\delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\sigma\gamma}\big)g_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\nu}\Big(\sqrt{-g}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)g_{\sigma\gamma}g^{\kappa\lambda}\Big)\Big]\,d^{4}x\,,

(C.1.24)

where we have used relations such as
\delta g_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}=-\delta g^{\sigma\gamma}g_{\sigma\alpha}g_{\gamma\beta}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}=\delta g^{\sigma\gamma}\partial_{\nu}g_{\sigma\gamma}
. Introducing the shorthand notation
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}:=\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{{\nu}}_{\kappa\lambda}
and expanding the partial derivatives, we have

\delta_{g}S_{\bar{\textrm{E}}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\sigma\gamma}\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\gamma}g^{\mu\lambda}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\lambda}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)+\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\sigma\gamma}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\sigma\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\gamma}\big)\\
-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\gamma}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\kappa\lambda}+\frac{1}{4}g_{\sigma\gamma}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\sigma\gamma}g_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\\
-\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\sigma\gamma}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g_{\sigma\gamma}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\sigma\gamma}g_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{4}g_{\sigma\gamma}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}g_{\sigma\gamma}g^{\kappa\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g_{\sigma\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}g_{\sigma\gamma}\partial_{\nu}g^{\kappa\lambda}\bigg]d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.25)

Lastly, making simplifications reduces the metric variations to

\displaystyle\delta_{g}S_{\bar{\textrm{E}}}

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\sigma\gamma}\Big[\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\sigma\gamma}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\sigma\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\gamma}\big)-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\gamma}g^{\mu\lambda}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\lambda}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)\\
+\partial_{\nu}\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\sigma\gamma}-\partial_{\sigma}\bar{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\lambda\gamma}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\gamma}g^{\kappa\lambda}(\partial_{\nu}\bar{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}-\partial_{\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda})\Big]d^{4}x\quad\end{multlined}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\sigma\gamma}\bar{G}_{\sigma\gamma}\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.27)

Hence we have obtained the metric-affine Einstein tensor from the metric variation of the bulk term. Also note that only the symmetric part contributes, due to the contraction with the (symmetric) metric variation.

The connection variations simply give rise to the Palatini tensor (
4.4.15
), which was already shown explicitly in equation (
4.4.25
). Substituting this into the action immediately gives

\displaystyle\delta_{\bar{\Gamma}}S_{\bar{\textrm{E}}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta_{\bar{\Gamma}}(\bar{\mathbf{G}})\,d^{4}x=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}P^{\mu\nu}{}_{\lambda}d^{4}x\,.

(C.1.28)

The total variations for the metric-affine Einstein action are therefore given by

\delta S_{\bar{\textrm{E}}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\Big[\delta g^{\mu\nu}\bar{G}_{\mu\nu}+\delta\bar{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}P^{\mu\nu}{}_{\lambda}\Big]d^{4}x\,,

(C.1.29)

as given in equation (
4.4.45
).

C.2
Modified actions

Here we calculate the equations of motion for the modified theories of Chapter
5
. Many of the results from the previous appendix are used, especially for the variations of the Levi-Civita and metric-affine bulk and boundary terms. These results can also be found in our work
[
222
,
173
]
.

C.2.1
f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})
action

For the fourth-order
f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})
action (
5.1.1
), the metric variations are

\displaystyle\delta S_{\textrm{grav}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\Big[\sqrt{-g}\delta f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})+\delta\sqrt{-g}f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})\Big]d^{4}x

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\Big[\frac{\partial f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})}{\partial\mathbf{G}}\delta\mathbf{G}+\frac{\partial f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})}{\partial\mathbf{B}}\delta\mathbf{B}\\
-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\delta g^{\mu\nu}f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})\Big]d^{4}x\,.\end{multlined}

We will split the calculation into two parts, focusing on the bulk terms and the boundary terms separately. The boundary term variations are notably more complicated, due to the presence of higher derivative terms. In this appendix we refrain from discussing boundary conditions, but see
[
222
]
for further details.

Bulk variations

In Appendix
C.1.1
we calculated the variation of the bulk term, so using equations (
C.1.4
) and (
C.1.11
) we can write

\displaystyle\delta\mathbf{G}=\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big(\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\lambda\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\sigma}-\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\lambda}+g_{\gamma\sigma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\delta g_{\alpha\beta}\,.

(C.2.3)

The first term of the integrand (
C.2.1
) is then

\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{G}}\delta\mathbf{G}=\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{G}}\Big[\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big(\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\lambda\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\sigma}-\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\gamma\lambda}+g_{\gamma\sigma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho}\Big)\\
+\frac{1}{2}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\delta g_{\alpha\beta}\Big]\,.

(C.2.4)

Expanding the definition of
M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho}
(
C.1.6
), we can write the final term in the curly brackets above as

\delta g^{\rho\sigma}g_{\gamma\sigma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho}=\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big(2g^{\nu\lambda}g_{\sigma\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\rho\lambda}-g^{\mu\nu}g_{\sigma\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\lambda\rho}-g^{\gamma\lambda}g_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\rho}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\lambda}\Big)\,.

(C.2.5)

Finally, the last term in (
C.2.4
) requires integration by parts, leading to

\frac{1}{2}\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{G}}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\delta g_{\alpha\beta,\gamma}d^{4}x=\textrm{`surface term'}-\frac{1}{2}\int\delta g_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\gamma}\big(\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{G}}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\big)d^{4}x\\
=\frac{1}{2}\int\Big[\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\gamma}(f_{,\mathbf{G}})E_{\rho\sigma}{}^{\gamma}+\delta g^{\alpha\beta}g_{\alpha\rho}g_{\beta\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{G}}\partial_{\gamma}(\sqrt{-g}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma})\Big]d^{4}x\,,

(C.2.6)

where the surface term proportional to
\delta g_{\alpha\beta}
will be dropped as usual. Note that we had already calculated
\partial_{\gamma}(\sqrt{-g}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma})
, so the second term in the final line of (
C.2.6
) is just
f_{,\mathbf{G}}
times our result in (
C.1.14
)

\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}g_{\alpha\rho}g_{\beta\sigma}\partial_{\gamma}f_{,\mathbf{G}}(\sqrt{-g}E^{\alpha\beta\gamma})=\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{G}}\Bigg[2\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\rho\sigma}-2\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\gamma\rho}\\
-g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}-2g_{\alpha\rho}g^{\epsilon\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\sigma\nu}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\gamma\epsilon}+g_{\alpha\rho}g^{\eta\gamma}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\gamma\eta}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}+g_{\alpha\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\gamma\sigma}\\
+g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\eta\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\eta}+\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\sigma}-\partial_{\rho}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\sigma\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\sigma\rho}g^{\kappa\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\gamma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\Bigg]\,.

(C.2.7)

From comparison with equation (
C.1.16
), we can immediately notice that the terms inside the square brackets of equations (
C.2.5
) and (
C.2.7
) almost give the Einstein tensor, but they are missing the variation of the metric determinant multiplied by
\mathbf{G}
(
C.1.3
). We therefore have that the variation of the bulk term is given by

\displaystyle\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{G}}\delta\mathbf{G}\,d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Big[f_{,\mathbf{G}}\Big(G_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}\mathbf{G}\big)+\frac{1}{2}E_{\rho\sigma}{}^{\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}f_{,\mathbf{G}}\Big]d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.8)

Boundary variations

The variation of the boundary term is

\displaystyle\delta\mathbf{B}=\delta\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)\partial_{\nu}\Big(\frac{\partial_{\mu}(gg^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\delta\Bigg(\partial_{\nu}\Big(\frac{\partial_{\mu}(gg^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)\Bigg)\,.

(C.2.9)

The first term simply gives the boundary term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sqrt{-g}}g_{\alpha\beta}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}\Big(\frac{\partial_{\mu}(gg^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)=\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}g_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{B}\,.

(C.2.10)

The second term we split into three calculations

\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\delta\Bigg(\partial_{\nu}\Big(\frac{\partial_{\mu}(gg^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)\Bigg)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\nu}\Big\{\overbrace{\frac{\partial_{\mu}(\delta gg^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}}^{1}+\overbrace{\frac{\partial_{\mu}(g\delta g^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}}^{2}\\
+\overbrace{\partial_{\mu}(gg^{\mu\nu})\delta\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)}^{3}\Big\}\,.

(C.2.11)

These three terms are computed to be the following

\qquad\quad\partial_{\nu}\Big[\overbrace{\frac{\partial_{\mu}(\delta gg^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}}^{1}\Big]=\partial_{\nu}\Bigg\{\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big[2\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}+2\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\alpha}g_{\eta\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\\
-g_{\alpha\beta}\big(\Gamma^{\mu}_{\mu\eta}g^{\eta\nu}+\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}g^{\mu\eta}\big)\Big]+\sqrt{-g}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\alpha\beta})\Bigg\}\,,

(C.2.12)

\quad\partial_{\nu}\Big[\overbrace{\frac{\partial_{\mu}(g\delta g^{\mu\nu})}{\sqrt{-g}}}^{2}\Big]=\partial_{\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{-g}\Big[2\delta g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}+\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\mu\nu})\Big]\Bigg]\,,\qquad\quad

(C.2.13)

\partial_{\nu}\bigg[\overbrace{\partial_{\mu}(gg^{\mu\nu})\delta\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\Big)}^{3}\bigg]=\partial_{\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big[\frac{g_{\alpha\beta}}{2}\big(\Gamma^{\mu}_{\mu\eta}g^{\eta\nu}+\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}g^{\mu\eta}\big)\\
-\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Big]\Bigg]\,.

(C.2.14)

Collecting these together gives the expression

\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\nu}\Bigg\{\sqrt{-g}\bigg[\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big(\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\eta}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}+2g_{\eta\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\alpha}-2\delta_{\alpha}^{\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\beta\gamma}\Big)\\
+g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\alpha\beta})-\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\mu\nu})\bigg]\Bigg\}\,.

(C.2.15)

The total variation of
\mathbf{B}
can then be written as

\delta\mathbf{B}=\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}g_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{B}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\nu}\Bigg\{\sqrt{-g}\bigg[\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big(\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\eta}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}+\\
2g_{\eta\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\alpha}-2\delta_{\alpha}^{\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\beta\gamma}\Big)+g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\alpha\beta})-\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\mu\nu})\bigg]\Bigg\}\,.

(C.2.16)

Substituting
\delta\mathbf{B}
(
C.2.16
) into the variational integral (
C.2.1
) yields

\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\delta\mathbf{B}\,d^{4}x=\int\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}g_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{B}\,d^{4}x\\
+f_{,\mathbf{B}}\partial_{\nu}\Bigg\{\sqrt{-g}\bigg[\overbrace{\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big(\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\eta}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}+2g_{\eta\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\alpha}-2\delta_{\alpha}^{\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\beta\gamma}\Big)}^{A}\\
+\overbrace{g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\alpha\beta})-\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\mu\nu})}^{B}\bigg]\Bigg\}\,d^{4}x\,,

(C.2.17)

where we perform integration by parts once on the
A
terms and twice on the
B
terms. For the
A
terms we have

\int f_{,\mathbf{B}}\partial_{\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big(\overbrace{\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\eta}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}+2g_{\eta\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\alpha}-2\delta_{\alpha}^{\nu}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\beta\gamma}}^{A}\Big)\Bigg]d^{4}x\\
=\textrm{`surface term'}-\int\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\overbrace{\big(...\big)}^{A}\sqrt{-g}\,d^{4}x\,,

(C.2.18)

where
\overbrace{\big(...\big)}^{A}
represents the terms underneath the brace on the previous line. For the
B
terms we have

\int f_{,\mathbf{B}}\partial_{\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{-g}\Big(\overbrace{g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\alpha\beta})-\partial_{\mu}(\delta g^{\mu\nu})}^{B}\Big)\Bigg]d^{4}x\\
=\textrm{`surface term'}-\int\partial_{\nu}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\sqrt{-g}\overbrace{\big(...\big)}^{B}\,d^{4}x\,,

(C.2.19)

Performing integration by parts once more leads to

\textrm{`surface terms'}+\int\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big[\partial_{\mu}\Big(\sqrt{-g}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\Big)-\partial_{\alpha}\big(\sqrt{-g}\partial_{\beta}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\big)\Big]d^{4}x\,,

(C.2.20)

where we now assume all surface terms proportional to
\delta g_{\mu\nu}
and
\partial\delta g_{\mu\nu}
vanish.

Expanding the partial derivatives of (
C.2.20
) gives

\int\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big[\partial_{\mu}\Big(\sqrt{-g}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\Big)-\partial_{\alpha}\big(\sqrt{-g}\partial_{\beta}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\big)\Big]d^{4}x\\
=\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\bigg[\partial_{\nu}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\Big[2g_{\eta\rho}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\sigma}-g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\eta}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}\Big]-\partial_{\sigma}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\Gamma^{\eta}_{\rho\eta}\\
+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}-\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\bigg]d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.21)

Putting equations (
C.2.18
) and (
C.2.21
) into our expression for the boundary variation (
C.2.17
), and cancelling off terms, we arrive at

\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\delta\mathbf{B}\,d^{4}x=\int\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\sqrt{-g}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}f_{,\mathbf{B}}g_{\rho\sigma}\mathbf{B}-\partial_{\nu}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\Big[\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu\eta}\\
+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\eta}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\mu\eta}-\delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\rho\gamma}\Big]+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})g_{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}-\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}(f_{,\mathbf{B}})\bigg]d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.22)

Lastly, let us rewrite the connection pieces in terms of partial derivatives of the metric

\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\delta\mathbf{B}\,d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\ g_{\rho\sigma}\mathbf{B}+g_{\rho\sigma}\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}f_{,\mathbf{B}}-\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\\
+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}(g^{\mu\nu})\partial_{\nu}f_{,\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{-g})\partial_{\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\bigg]d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.23)

Full variation

The full variation of the gravitational
f(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{B})
action is the sum of the variation of the bulk term (
C.2.8
), the boundary term (
C.2.23
) and the metric determinant

\delta S_{\textrm{grav}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\rho\sigma}\Bigg[f_{,\mathbf{G}}\Big(G_{\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}\mathbf{G}\big)+\frac{1}{2}E_{\rho\sigma}{}^{\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}f_{,\mathbf{G}}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}f\\
+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{B}+g_{\rho\sigma}\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}f_{,\mathbf{B}}-\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\\
+\frac{1}{2}g_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}(g^{\mu\nu})\partial_{\nu}f_{,\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{-g})\partial_{\sigma}f_{,\mathbf{B}}\Bigg]\,d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.24)

Note that the equations are symmetric over
\rho
and
\sigma
. This is the result given in equation (
5.1.5
).

C.2.2
f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})
action

Now let us derive the field equations for the modified metric-affine action of Sec.
5.3
. Here we will compute the variations with respect to the metric and independent connection simultaneously. For the action (
5.3.1
) the variations lead to

\displaystyle\delta S_{\textrm{grav}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\Big[\delta\sqrt{-g}f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})+\sqrt{-g}\delta f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})\Big]d^{4}x

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\Big[-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\delta g^{\mu\nu}f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})\\
+\frac{\partial f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})}{\partial\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\delta\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\frac{\partial f(\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{B}})}{\partial\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\delta\bar{\mathbf{B}}\Big]d^{4}x\,.\end{multlined}

Using the previous calculations from Appendix
C.1.2
, and again using the shorthand notation
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}:=\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\delta^{\nu}_{\kappa}-\bar{\Gamma}^{{\nu}}_{\kappa\lambda}
, the variation of the bulk term is

\delta\bar{\mathbf{G}}=\delta g^{\mu\lambda}\big(\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\kappa\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\lambda}-\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\rho}\bar{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\kappa\lambda}\big)+\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}\delta g^{\lambda\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\lambda\kappa}g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\delta g_{\alpha\beta}g^{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}g_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\nu}_{\kappa\lambda}+\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}P^{\mu\nu}{}_{\lambda}\,,

(C.2.27)

where the final two terms on the first line will require integration by parts. We can write this in a nicer form by recalling that for
f(\bar{\mathbf{G}})=\bar{\mathbf{G}}
the metric variations, along with the variation of the metric determinant, give the metric-affine Einstein tensor. The only additional terms for the modified action will come from integration by parts when the partial derivatives hit the
f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}
term in (
C.2.2
). In other words, we have

\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\delta_{g}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\,d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}\Big(\delta g^{\mu\nu}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\bar{G}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\mu\nu}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}g_{\mu\nu}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\\
+\delta g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\rho\kappa}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\kappa\rho}-\tilde{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\big)\Big)d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.28)

Also recall that the bulk term can be written in the form given in (
4.1.16
) where all the derivative terms are included in
\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\lambda}g^{\mu\nu}\bar{E}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}
. Therefore it must be the case that this final term is proportional to
\bar{E}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}
, which is easily verified

\displaystyle\delta g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\rho\kappa}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\kappa\rho}-\tilde{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\kappa\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\rho}_{\rho\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\kappa\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\lambda}_{\rho\kappa}+\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}-\delta^{\lambda}_{\mu}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\rho}_{\rho\nu}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\bar{E}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}\,.

(C.2.29)

In total, for the bulk term we have

\displaystyle\int\sqrt{-g}

\displaystyle f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\delta\bar{\mathbf{G}}\,d^{4}x

\displaystyle=\int\sqrt{-g}\delta g^{\mu\nu}\Big(f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\bar{G}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}g_{\mu\nu}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}\bar{E}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}\Big)d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.30)

For the boundary term (
4.1.9
) we have

\delta\bar{\mathbf{B}}=\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\bar{\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\kappa}\Bigg[\sqrt{-g}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}(g^{\mu\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}-g^{\kappa\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\mu}{}_{\mu\lambda})\\
+\delta g^{\mu\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}-\delta g^{\kappa\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\Big)\Bigg]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\kappa}\Big(\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\lambda}\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}-\sqrt{-g}g^{\kappa\lambda}\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\Big)\,,

(C.2.31)

where all but the first term require integration by parts. It is clear that if
f
is a linear function of
\bar{\mathbf{B}}
then this term is a pure boundary term and does not contribute to the equations of motion. Plugging these into the variations (
C.2.2
) and discarding boundary terms we obtain

\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\delta\bar{\mathbf{B}}\,d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}\Bigg\{\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big[\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}+(\partial_{\kappa}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}})\Big(\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}(g^{\mu\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}\\
-g^{\kappa\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda})-\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\alpha\beta}+\delta^{\kappa}_{\alpha}\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\mu}_{\mu\beta}\Big)\Big]+\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}(\delta^{\mu}_{\kappa}g^{\lambda\nu}-\delta^{\lambda}_{\kappa}g^{\mu\nu})\Bigg\}d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.32)

The final terms on the first line are exactly the terms found in (
C.2.2
), with the opposite signs, so we can write this as

\int\sqrt{-g}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\delta\bar{\mathbf{B}}\,d^{4}x=\int\sqrt{-g}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\delta g^{\alpha\beta}\Big(g_{\alpha\beta}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}-\partial_{\kappa}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\bar{E}_{\alpha\beta}{}^{\kappa}\Big)\\
+2\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}g^{\nu[\lambda}\delta^{\mu]}_{\kappa}\Big]d^{4}x\,.

(C.2.33)

Combining these results (
C.2.28
) and (
C.2.33
), the full variations of the modified action are

\delta S_{\textrm{grav}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\sqrt{-g}\bigg\{\delta g^{\mu\nu}\Big[f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}(\bar{G}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\bar{\mathbf{G}})-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}f+\frac{1}{2}\bar{E}_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}(\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}-\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}})\\
+\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\Big]+\delta\bar{\Gamma}{}^{\kappa}_{\mu\nu}\Big(P^{\mu\nu}{}_{\kappa}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{G}}}+2\partial_{\lambda}f_{,\bar{\mathbf{B}}}g^{\nu[\lambda}\delta^{\mu]}_{\kappa}\Big)\bigg\}d^{4}x\,,

(C.2.34)

which are given in (
5.3.2
).

Appendix D
Stueckelberg trick

Here we show that the Stueckelberg trick applied to the Einstein action and metric bulk term
\mathbf{G}
leads directly to the non-metricity scalar
Q
for the symmetric teleparallel connection. This verifies the calculation in equation (
4.3
), and also shows that the gauge boundary term
b_{\xi}
(
3.3.29
) can be written in a form that is quadratic in the affine connection.

The bulk term transforms under an infinitesimal coordinate transformation as (
4.1.2
), where the additional inhomogeneous
M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\lambda}
term appears. In order to apply the Stueckelberg trick, however, we want the full general coordinate transformation. By a similar calculation it is not too difficult to see that
\mathbf{G}
transforms as

\mathbf{G}(x)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{G}}(\hat{x})=\mathbf{G}(x)+2g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\alpha\gamma}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\kappa}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}-g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\lambda\kappa}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\kappa}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\\
-g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\kappa}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}}{\partial x^{\gamma}}+g^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\kappa}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\kappa}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}}{\partial x^{\gamma}}\\
-g^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\kappa}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\kappa}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}}{\partial x^{\gamma}}\,.

(D.1)

This can then be simplified as follows

\hat{\mathbf{G}}(\hat{x})=\mathbf{G}+M^{\beta\kappa}{}_{\gamma}\Big(\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\kappa}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\Big)\\
+2g^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\kappa}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}}\frac{\partial^{2}x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\kappa}}\Big(\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{[\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda]}}{\partial x^{\gamma}}\Big)\ .

(D.2)

As one would expect, the infinitesimal limit in (
4.1.2
) is obtained immediately by using
\hat{x}^{\mu}=x^{\mu}+\xi^{\mu}(x)
.
Next we make various simplifications by applying the chain rule on the second-order Jacobian terms

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\mathbf{G}-M^{\beta\kappa}{}_{\gamma}\Big(\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\eta}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\eta}\partial x^{\kappa}}\Big)\\
+2g^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial x^{\kappa}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\eta}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda}}\frac{\partial x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial x^{\sigma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\nu}}\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\eta}\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\sigma}\partial x^{\kappa}}\Big(\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{[\nu}}{\partial x^{\beta}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\lambda]}}{\partial x^{\gamma}}\Big)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\mathbf{G}-M^{\beta\kappa}{}_{\gamma}\Big(\frac{\partial x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{\kappa}}\Big)\\
+g^{\alpha\beta}\big(\delta^{\sigma}_{\beta}\delta^{\eta}_{\gamma}-\delta^{\eta}_{\beta}\delta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}\big)\frac{\partial x^{\kappa}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\mu}}\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{x}^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\eta}\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial x^{\gamma}}{\partial\hat{x}^{\rho}}\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{x}^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\sigma}\partial x^{\kappa}}\,.\end{multlined}

The next step is to promote the new coordinate to the Stueckelberg fields
\hat{x}^{\mu}\rightarrow\xi^{\mu}
, see for example
[
348
]
for details, which gives

\hat{\mathbf{G}}=\mathbf{G}-M^{\beta\kappa}{}_{\gamma}\Big(\frac{\partial x^{\gamma}}{\partial\xi^{\mu}}\frac{\partial^{2}\xi^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\beta}\partial x^{\kappa}}\Big)+g^{\alpha\beta}\big(\delta^{\sigma}_{\beta}\delta^{\eta}_{\gamma}-\delta^{\eta}_{\beta}\delta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}\big)\frac{\partial x^{\kappa}}{\partial\xi^{\mu}}\frac{\partial^{2}\xi^{\mu}}{\partial x^{\eta}\partial x^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial x^{\gamma}}{\partial\xi^{\rho}}\frac{\partial^{2}\xi^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\sigma}\partial x^{\kappa}}\,.

(D.6)

Now all that is left is to identify the terms above with the symmetric teleparallel connection (
3.3.21
), which we remind the reader is defined as

\bar{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{\partial x^{\lambda}}{\partial\xi^{\rho}}\frac{\partial^{2}\xi^{\rho}}{\partial x^{\mu}\partial x^{\nu}}\,.

We are therefore led to the final expression for the transformation of the bulk term

\hat{\mathbf{G}}=\mathbf{G}-M^{\beta\kappa}{}_{\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\beta\kappa}+2g^{\alpha\beta}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\alpha[\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\beta]\kappa}\,.

(D.7)

Finally, we will equate these additional terms with the non-metricity gauge boundary term
b_{\xi}
defined in (
3.3.29
), which is given by

\displaystyle b_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\partial_{\kappa}\big(\sqrt{-g}g^{\lambda[\mu}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa]}_{\mu\lambda}\big)

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=2\Gamma^{\rho}_{\kappa\rho}g^{\lambda[\mu}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa]}_{\mu\lambda}-\big(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\eta}g^{\eta\mu}+\Gamma^{\mu}_{\kappa\eta}g^{\lambda\eta}\big)\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}+\big(\Gamma^{\kappa}_{\kappa\eta}g^{\eta\lambda}+\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\kappa\eta}g^{\eta\kappa}\big)\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\\
+g^{\lambda\mu}\partial_{\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}-g^{\lambda\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}\,,\end{multlined}

where we remind the reader that we are working with both the Levi-Civita connection
\Gamma
and the symmetric teleparallel connection
\bar{\Gamma}
. There are two ways to deal with the derivative terms on the final line and rewrite them in terms of quadratic connection pieces. The first is to use the explicit form of the affine connection in terms of
\xi^{\mu}
, where one would easily find all the third derivatives of
\xi
cancelling via symmetry, with quadratic terms in
\bar{\Gamma}
remaining. A quicker route is to recall the teleparallel condition
\bar{R}=0
, such that derivatives of the affine connection can be related to quadratic connection terms. Either way, we obtain an expression for the derivative terms

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\mu}\partial_{\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\mu\lambda}-g^{\lambda\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\lambda}=g^{\kappa\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\eta\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\eta}_{\mu\lambda}-g^{\mu\lambda}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\eta\kappa}\bar{\Gamma}^{\eta}_{\mu\lambda}\,.

(D.10)

Using this result along with the definition of
M^{\mu\nu}{}_{\lambda}
shows that the boundary term (
D
) can be written as

b_{\xi}=-M^{\beta\kappa}{}_{\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\beta\kappa}+2g^{\alpha\beta}\bar{\Gamma}^{\kappa}_{\alpha[\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\beta]\kappa}\,.

(D.11)

In total, we find that applying the Stueckelberg procedure to restore the invariance of the bulk term
\mathbf{G}
leads exactly to the non-metricity scalar of symmetric teleparallel gravity

\hat{\mathbf{G}}=\mathbf{G}+b_{\xi}=-Q(g,\xi)\,,

(D.12)

as reported in equation (
4.3
).

Appendix E
Dynamical systems stability analysis

Here we include some further details for the stability analysis of the dynamical systems of Section
6.3
. This material is adapted from our works
[
223
,
224
]
. Firstly, we perform the stability analysis for the general fixed points of the second-order modified cosmologies. We then study the stability analysis of the Anagnostopoulos et al. model Sec.
6.2.3
, which has a nonhyperbolic fixed point. We must therefore use techniques beyond linear stability theory in this case.

E.1
Stability analysis of the general system

The critical points of system (
6.2.10
)–(
6.2.11
), as given in Table
6.1
, depend on the explicit form of
n(Z)
(
6.2.9
) and
m(z)
(
6.2.8
). These functions themselves depend on the choice of function
f
.
Nevertheless, it is possible to make certain general statements about the nature of the critical points. The stability matrix or Jacobian is given by

\displaystyle J=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\frac{\partial X_{2}^{\prime}}{\partial X_{2}}&\displaystyle\frac{\partial X_{2}^{\prime}}{\partial Z}\\[8.0pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\partial Z^{\prime}}{\partial X_{2}}&\displaystyle\frac{\partial Z^{\prime}}{\partial Z}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(E.1.1)

We denote the two eigenvalues of
J
by
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
.

For the points in Table
6.1
, the necessary existence conditions shall be assumed, but care must be taken with the derivative of the function
m(Z)
, which will contain third derivatives of
f
(the derivatives of
n(Z)
can be rewritten in terms of
m
and
n
). It is then often necessary to assume that
f^{(3)}
, which appears in the numerator of the elements of the Jacobian matrix, remains finite at the fixed point in order to determine the stability for a general
f
. We will make this assumption explicit when needed.

For P
{}_{1}=(0,0)
one obtains the eigenvalues

\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{P}_{1}}=\frac{2-4m(0)-3n(0)}{1+m(0)}\qquad,\qquad\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{P}_{1}}=\frac{3n(0)-6}{1+m(0)}\,,

(E.1.2)

which has at least one negative eigenvalue. Conditions on
m(Z)
and
n(Z)
could then be imposed to determine its stability.
The eigenvalues of point P
{}_{2}=(0,1)
takes a similar form but with
m
and
n
evaluated at
Z=1

\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{P}_{2}}=\frac{2-4m(1)-3n(1)}{1+m(1)}\qquad,\qquad\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{P}_{2}}=-\frac{3n(1)-6}{1+m(1)}\,.

(E.1.3)

Unsurprisingly, with
m
and
n
undefined for these critical points, there is little that can be said about their stability in full generality.

For the point P
{}_{n}=(0,Z^{*})
with
n(Z^{*})=2
one obtains constant eigenvalues

\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{P}_{n}}=-4\qquad,\qquad\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{P}_{n}}=-3\,,

(E.1.4)

meaning this point is always stable when it exists. This is consistent with its interpretation as being a late-time de Sitter attractor with
q=-1
. The stability of the point P
{}_{m}=(0,Z^{*})
with
m(Z^{*})\rightarrow\infty
depends on the third derivatives of
f
, so one cannot make definitive claims about its stability. However, if one checks that both
n(Z^{*})
and
f^{(3)}(Z^{*})
remain finite while
m(Z^{*})\rightarrow\infty
, the eigenvalues will also be
\lambda_{1}=-4
and
\lambda_{2}=-3
, indicating stability. This would be consistent with the interpretation of P
m
as a stable, late-time, de Sitter point. Note that in other cases (such as for the Born-Infeld models of Sec.
6.1
) the point P
m
is instead unstable, and acts as an early-time inflationary fixed point. It should therefore be emphasised that the properties and interpretation of P
m
is model dependent.

Point A
=(X_{2}^{*},0)
, with
X_{2}^{*}
defined previously, has eigenvalues

\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{A}}=-4\qquad,\qquad\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{A}}=-\frac{2-4m(0)-3n(0)}{1+m(0)}\,.

(E.1.5)

The second eigenvalue is just minus the first of point P
1
’s in (
E.1.2
), therefore both points cannot be stable simultaneously. For point B the eigenvalues depend on the higher derivatives of
f
, so again, the stability cannot be determined without specifying a model
f
. If all derivatives of
f
stay finite at
Z=1
it is possible to at least conclude that this point must be unstable, as the eigenvalues cannot both be negative. Once again, this will be highly model-dependent.

E.2
Stability analysis of the Anagnostopoulos et al. model

The fixed points for the model of Anagnostopoulos et al. are given in Table
6.3
. The stability has also been included, with each point having the following eigenvalues determined by the methods outlined above. Using the functions
m(Z)
and
n(Z)
for this model, given in equations (
6.2.24
) and (
6.2.25
) respectively, we find
[
224
]

•

Point P
2
has eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{P}_{2}}=-1
,
\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{P}_{2}}=3
and is a saddle.

•

Point B has eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{B}}=1
,
\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{B}}=4
and is unstable.

•

Point P
n
has eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{P}_{n}}=-4
,
\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{P}_{n}}=-3
and is stable.

•

Point P
m
has eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}^{\textrm{P}_{m}}=0
,
\lambda_{2}^{\textrm{P}_{m}}=-4
and is nonhyperbolic.

A closer look at the nonhyperbolic point is shown in Fig.
E.1
, the orange region representing the physical phase space as determined from the Hubble constraint (
6.2.5
). Recall that we require
\lambda<0
for the existence of this point. Despite the fact that the point appears to be mathematically unstable, with trajectories moving away from the point in Fig.
E.1
, the Hubble constraint can be used to determine the fate of trajectories within the physical phase space.

Figure E.1
:
Nonhyperbolic fixed point P
m
.

The boundary of the physical phase space is described by the equations

X_{2}=e^{\lambda\big(\frac{1}{Z}-1\big)}\Big(1+\frac{2\lambda(Z-1)}{Z}\Big)\quad,\quad\text{with}\ X_{2}\geq 0\ ,\ 0\leq Z\leq 1\,\ ,\ {\lambda<0}\,,

(E.2.1)

see
[
224
]
for details.
As
Z
approaches zero (from above)
X_{2}
goes to zero. One can also show that for all
X_{2}\geq 0
, trajectories always travel in the negative
Z
direction whilst
Z
is between
0
and
1
. This can be most easily seen by substituting the expression for
X_{2}
on the physical boundary (
E.2.1
) into the autonomous equation
dZ/dN
. This resulting equation is

\frac{dZ}{dN}=-\frac{4Z^{2}(1-Z)\big(Z-2\lambda(1-Z)\big)}{Z^{2}+2\lambda^{2}(Z-1)^{2}-\lambda Z(1-Z)}\,.

(E.2.2)

All terms in the numerator and denominator are positive for
\lambda<0
, therefore
dZ/dN
is negative and trajectories on the boundary approach the origin.

Following the same logic, the same result can be shown for the general equation
dZ/dN
with
\lambda<0
. We can therefore conclude that all physical trajectories satisfying the Hubble constraint travel towards and terminate at the origin, point P
m
. This is because trajectories are smooth and do not cross the boundary, and therefore terminate at
Z=0
with
X_{2}=0
. This indeed matches what can be observed in the phase portraits, Fig.
6.6(b)
and
E.1
.
